## ⭐ Memory architecture — the addressable-memory thesis (NEW, wave-19)

> **Context.** Wave-19 named the architecture the program has been building without naming (CLU = an *addressable dynamical memory*) and measured which half of it works. **Write, read, durability, locality and hard read isolation all work — under DESIGN, with zero learning.** *Indexing* them by gradient does not (**N61**), and the pieces that would *use* them do not exist. **This section is the not-yet-attempted register for that architecture; the tried-and-failed half is N61–N65.**
> ⚠ **Standing scope guard on everything below:** the demonstrated loop is **hand-designed**. Nothing here is evidence of emergence, and N46/CM-16a say the emergent counterparts do **not** appear.

- **⭐⭐ The d-dimensional address-space scaling test — *the cheapest decisive follow-up in the program*.** *What we haven't shown:* whether address capacity grows with latent dimension as the packing bound predicts. *Why it matters:* the measured ceiling of **8 items** is a **2-D ring** property (`K_max ≈ 0.2·2π/σ_θ` → 8.4 predicted, 8 measured — *query precision vs site spacing*), while the theorist's packing bound is **`(1+2R/w)^d`, exponential in `d`**. **It decides whether "a handful of items" is a property of the 2-D toy or of the primitive — the difference between an interesting memory and a non-starter.** ⚠ **Both w19 agents named it independently and both refused to let the 8 be quoted as CLU's capacity** (watch-item **W2**, for which this test is the explicit promote-to-negative trigger). *Where it lands:* gates every capacity sentence in any memory paper; ICLR long. Status: **`→ SHOWN (wave-20, address-space-dimension-scaling)` — and CLU won it: `K_max = 4·2^d` exactly over d=2–8 (R²=1.0000, 1024 items at d=8, blanks passing), the 8-item ceiling is DEAD, W2 CLEARED, no death zone at any d.** ⚠ Three inheritances: the packing bound itself was **falsified in form** (N70 — use `Δ_req ≈ 3.1·max(w,σ_query)`, `d_eff ≈ 0.72–0.83·d`); the result is **designed-landscape** (the learned-content curve is rising but unpinned, N82 — see the dimension-aware-budget re-run below); and capacity-per-parameter is still a losing comparison (N77 — carry the denominator).
- **⭐ The *derived-address retrieval gate* — the named minimal falsifier of the whole architecture.** *What we haven't shown:* that the surviving components **compose**. Spec (pre-registered by its author): hand-designed K=4 landscape, payload ≥16 reals (local spectrum + register values), address dim 5; (1) selective retrieval under a **time-averaged linear read** (Toy A says it passes); (2) **derived addressing** — `q₀(query)` = the endpoint of a short **damped relaxation** from the query embedding (content-addressable, *no gradient through dynamics*), `p₀`/`m` from a head refined **within-basin only** (the measured-working regime), plus a **boost-retry ladder** (≤R retries over increasing `‖p₀‖`) as the **sole cross-basin mechanism**; (3) train the selector/writer under the **payload>address bottleneck** and run the identity-rollout ablation. **Pre-register: end-to-end retrieval ≥80% at K=4.** *Why it matters:* each component is individually measured or trivially robust, so **joint failure would mean the components do not compose — the last way the read side can fail.** ⛔ **Do NOT gate it on gradient-based cross-basin address search: that is measured dead (4 protocols) and gating on it would re-test a known negative.** Status: `OPEN` (specced in `clu-memory-architecture` §8; *"this is `clu-retrieval-demo` + one added stage; their harness is the right vehicle"*). ⚠ **w20 additions that bind on it:** the retry stage must be **codebook-gated** (naive score-gating destroys 2/267 correct reads, proven — N71); the address derivation must use **γ>0 relaxation + a `λ_min(H)>0` check, never a critical-point solver** (the Newton-saddle guard, N71); and **`a* = relax(c)` has still NEVER been implemented under learning** — w20's E1 was satisfied by a static objective that *enforces* coincidence rather than deriving it (Hub's own E1 adjudication), so the derived-address write rule remains untested. That last item is arguably its own cheap experiment.
- **⭐ THE CONTROLLER — the architecture's single largest hole.** *What we haven't shown:* **anything at all.** Every verb in the Head's vision — *decide, triage, add, create, trash, select, combine* — is a controller verb, and **none is built**. What decides *what to write where, with what mass, and how to read it back* does not exist. Nearest prior structure in the program is the address selector, which is exactly the piece that failed. *Why it matters:* the physics is demonstrated and the components are inventoried; **the controller is what turns a set of mechanisms into an architecture.** *Where it lands:* the architectural spine of any memory paper; ICLR long. Status: **`→ SHOWN (wave-23, controller-mvp)` for MVC-0 — the hole is no longer empty, and the result is two-part.** Every primitive verb is built, hand-coded, no learning (`chlu/core/controller.py`): **admission** (spacing gate `d_safe=4.4s` + refuse-and-relocate; the gate now **demonstrably fires** — live-site spacing 1.61 ≥ 1.54, intervention 0.20→0.97 over K, contrast N74's vacuous gate), **placement** (derived address, no search), **eviction** (LRU/depth, permanent never evicted, all-permanent store raises a capacity alarm rather than overwriting), **decay** (leaky wells `amp·exp(−leak·t)`, self-evict below floor; permanent + leaky coexist in ONE store). N75 rematch: **per-admitted 1.000 flat to K=64, best of five**; **per-offered 0.081 (fixed geometry, LAST of seven) but 0.669 (sized geometry, BEATS all four primitives)** ⇒ *the address space, not the controller, is the binding constraint* (**N91**). ⚠ **Still `OPEN` beyond MVC-0:** a *learned* policy (this is hand-coded by design), the controller on a **learned** landscape (the spacing certificate is meaningless for a global-support write, N75), `on_evict` under a binding budget (never stressed on the rematch curve — budget 6 ≥ admitted 5.2), and the *combine/triage* verbs, which remain unbuilt.
- **⭐ The I/O gap — a learned read-in `φ` and a learned read-out `ψ`.** *What we haven't shown:* CLU operating on a *learned representation* rather than raw sensors. Measured today (`clu-latent-io-audit` §Item 2): **`φ` is ≈ the IDENTITY** (`q₀` = a literal z-scored sensor frame, `p₀` = a literal scaled first difference — **there is NO random initial latent anywhere**, at training or inference: the Head's specific concern is *disconfirmed*, close that line), **`ψ` is HANDCRAFTED** (energy, `‖∇V‖`, relaxation residual, `q*`), so **all representational burden falls on `V_θ` alone**. *Why it matters:* **this one gap predicts most of the program's negatives** — 11/16 physics scalars at chance, best-config-is-least-dynamics, and the rollout-below-persistence result (with identity `φ` we tested CLU as a **raw-sensor forecaster**). ⚠ **And the hoped-for escape is NOT yet supported:** the feature-space persistence gate is **worse** for CLU (loses at every horizon by **20–56×**, on **30/30 features at n=1**), because `ψ` is quadratic in a state that free-streams away at 6.9–12.2×. **Hold "we were unfairly measured as a raw forecaster" as a hypothesis about a future learned `ψ`, never as a defence of the current one.** Status: **`φ` half `→ SHOWN (wave-23, phi-read-in)`; `ψ` half still `OPEN`.** ⭐ **The premise is validated:** a real learned `φ` (frozen PCA **and** a separately-trained AE, both fit on a disjoint pool, never through the store) lifts CLU from **CIFAR chance 0.012 → 0.81–0.97** and rescues closed-form Hopfield from raw chance too; CLU-in-φ ≫ Hopfield-in-φ at load/noise (CIFAR M=256: **0.973 vs 0.008**). ⛔ **But the hoped-for consequence did NOT follow:** with `φ` in place the designed store **still never beats kNN-in-φ** — the laundering control fires on all four cells, max CLU margin **0.000** (**N89**) ⇒ *"it only loses because there's no embedding" is closed as a defence.* ⚠ And R19-1's resolution (w23) removes the *other* half of this entry's hope: the persistence loss is **not** a protocol artifact — under the honest **L-1** launch CLU loses at every horizon (0.7705 vs 0.5669), with `φ`=identity or not (**N86 upheld**). ⇒ **the remaining live piece is the learned `ψ` read-out** (`clu-horizon-encoder`, still UNWRITTEN), plus φ at higher `phi_dim`/other families/multi-seed, and an `s_frac` capacity↔noise Pareto in φ-space (the shipped rule `s=0.3·median-NN` sits **exactly at the packing bound**, slack ≡1.08, **no headroom**).
- **Key/value separation — a first-order design question raised by prior art.** *What we haven't shown:* an address embedding **distinct from the launch state**. *Why it matters:* Csordás & Schmidhuber (ICLR 2019) rank **key/value entanglement** as DNC's *"most important"* failure — value contamination makes the content-lookup address distribution *noisy and flat* — and ⚠ **in CLU `q₀` is simultaneously the key AND the initial condition of the value, so the defect is ARCHITECTURAL rather than incidental.** Their fixes bought a **43%** bAbI mean-error improvement, i.e. these were the dominant defects and were fixable *within* soft addressing. *Where it lands:* recommended to the physics-theorist as a first-order design question. Status: `OPEN` (scout-recommended, unfunded).
- **Per-launch mass override — the one code change the architecture cannot proceed without.** *What we haven't shown:* masses as **per-address codebook entries**. Today `log_mass` is a **global model parameter shared by every rollout** ⇒ **`m` is not an address component at all**, and D1 freezes it as gauge. Prop 6 proves the escape: with per-item masses only the overall *scale* stays gauge and **all ratios are wake-visible and exactly learnable (2.2e-14)**. Spec: `CHLU.__call__(q0, p0, steps, dt, gamma, mass_override=None)` threading a per-rollout `M` into `T` without touching the trainable global. Status: **the prerequisite is `→ SHOWN (wave-20, mass-visible-objective)` — per-launch `mass_override` is implemented and shipped (default `None` = bit-identical), and it is what measured `τ ∝ M^0.779 (R²=0.9968)` on a fixed landscape; the full per-address CODEBOOK remains a change-list (`OPEN`).** ⚠ Scope sharpened at w21 (Prop F1/N76): per-address masses are a **selection** mechanism (which shell/timescale reads) — they store nothing; do not motivate this entry as a capacity channel.
- **Distillation training of the address selector.** *What we haven't shown:* the selector trained as an **amortization of content-addressable relaxation** (Hopfield-exact at γ>0) rather than by search. *Why it matters:* it is a concrete training story that **avoids backprop-through-relaxation entirely — which Prop 5 forbids anyway** — and it is the surviving learnability route after N61. Status: `THEORY-ONLY` (derived, unattempted).
- **The decorative-CLU falsifier: the payload>address information budget.** *What we haven't shown:* a task where the rollout is **load-bearing by counting**. Mechanism: the address carries ≤ `(2d + d_m)` reals of query-dependent information through the `φ` bottleneck; if the answer requires `≫` that, the excess bits **can only enter the trajectory from `V_θ`**. Enforce by dim-capping `φ`'s output to the address alone (no side channel to `ψ`) and letting `ψ` see only the trajectory. Certifying ablations: **identity-rollout must FAIL**, **random-V must FAIL**, **trained-V + shuffled codebook must FAIL selectively**. ⚠ **Honest note carried from the source: on FD001 today CLU fails test (i) in spirit** (a raw-stats encoder at **0.7486** beats every CLU arm) — **the current pipeline IS decorative**, which is precisely the architectural-gap diagnosis. *Why it matters:* it makes "is the physics doing anything?" **falsifiable** rather than rhetorical. Status: `OPEN`.
- **Basin-violation-on-hand-off — a free, cheap, pre-registrable experiment handed to us by prior art.** Kong et al. (2024) report that retrieval **fails when the carried-over state lies outside the target attractor's basin** on an index switch. ⚠ **This is the predicted failure mode for CLU's multi-particle consolidation and wormhole hand-offs**, and it is directly testable. Status: `OPEN` (scout-flagged as *"a free gift"*).
- **The washboard-death-zone curriculum hazard.** *What we haven't shown:* what happens when item density **increases during training**. The three-regime capacity picture predicts it passes **through the worst regime** (K≈16, selectivity 0.49) on the way to the continuum register — a curriculum hazard for any write controller. Status: `OPEN` (theorist OQ-D).
- **Untested address-key arms (from the theorist's urgent note to the engineer).** Scalar mass at `p₀=0` **provably cannot discriminate** (Prop 1, exact √M time dilation, verified 1.07e-4 Newtonian *and* relativistic). Three arms make mass a real key and **none is measured**: (a) **`p₀ ≠ 0`** energy gating; (b) **anisotropic per-channel masses** (breaks the dilation); (c) **time-/frequency-resolved reads** (frequency ratio = `√(M₂/M₁)`, verified to 0.2%). Also untested: a **full learned diagonal `M`** over a higher-dimensional latent. ⚠ **Condition added at w21 (Prop F1, N76):** arm (b) is gauge-equivalent to reshaping `V` whenever `V` is **separable** — mass ratios are identifiable **iff the landscape couples the coordinates** (measured 8.6 bits coupled vs 0.20 separable), and the relativistic governor is the third separator (it gauge-fixes `M` against `V`). Any mass-key experiment must use a coupled landscape or a momentum-carrying launch, and must claim **selection**, never storage. Status: `OPEN`.
- **Repair options for the address staircase that were never run.** Only the γ-anneal was tried (and failed). Untested: (2) **a separate informative low-γ search objective kept distinct from the high-γ read** (the γ=0 gradient is 3.3e-1, four orders larger — the failed anneal crudely mixed them *inside one loss*); (3) **softening the basins** so the address landscape is convex (⚠ may have no feasible window — at `barrier=0.05` the well could not hold a jittered query at all, payload error 0.18 at K=8; *worth one cheap sweep*); (4) **content-addressable relaxation** instead of continuous address search. Status: `OPEN`.
- **Relativistic scope check on Props 4/5.** All toys are Newtonian except Toy B's relativistic arm. The Prop 4/5 arguments are **kinetic-agnostic** (they rest on symplecticity of the splitting), but **relativistic mode-coupling at finite `p` (F5 Prop-2) could shorten regular horizons** — untested. Status: `OPEN` (cheap, only if it matters).
- **Does the cross-basin negative worsen with dimension?** The identified mechanism (separatrix roughness) **predicts the negative gets worse at scale** — which is itself a testable prediction, and one that would convert a protocol-level negative into a structural one. ⚠ Also un-run within the existing family: a **horizon-annealed** or **population-averaged** GD arm. Status: `OPEN` (theorist OQ-A).

## ⭐ Performance & the benchmark frontier (waves 20–22 fold, added 2026-07-23 by `doc-curator-w20-w22-sync`)

> **Context.** The Head reset the scoring at w22 (binding): **performance is the deliverable; the score = external benchmarks won on their own headline metric — currently ZERO.** Filing rule: property-demo without performance → workshop shorts; performance without explanation → ICLR; performance + explaining law → ICLR/NMI; **do not pre-register the retreat**. After three performance tasks: the primitive claim survived its sharpest test (N82), exactly **one** performance edge survives (capacity, 2.1× vs matched GRU/SSM — N78), and two capabilities have no Hopfield analogue (retry, per-item retention — N81). Everything below is the *not-yet-attempted* half of that frontier.

- **⭐⭐ The retry / test-time-compute study — the most promising novel-property-with-teeth in the program.** *What we haven't shown:* the +46.9pp retry (0.492→0.961 at ×1.5 compute, blank −38.3pp — `hopfield-capacity-benchmark` §4.1) elevated into a real **accuracy-vs-compute curve on a task where thinking-longer helps**, against a feedforward baseline that structurally cannot draw the curve (deeper boost ladders, confidence thresholds swept, seeds). *Why it matters:* it is the program's first demonstrated novel-property-WITH-performance, squarely in the field's hottest area (test-time compute), and w19's novelty scout confirmed the mechanism unoccupied. Files as a short now; **ICLR the moment it carries a benchmark**. ⚠ Retry acceptance must be codebook/confidence-gated (the −38pp blank IS the safety result; N71's guard is the designed-store analogue). Status: **`→ SHOWN (wave-23, retry-compute-study)` — the curve exists and the MECHANISM survives every control; the benchmark win does not.** Delivered: a monotone, **auto-stopping** accuracy-vs-compute curve (+6.6…+76.2 pp, saturating at ×1.2–1.8) with **equal-energy random-kick and ensemble-of-k dead flat in all 8 cells** (the directed boost is the mechanism), a load-bearing confidence gate (ungated collapses 0.96→0.004; τ=0.99 optimum), and the anchored **RUD-C benchmark spec** with four mandatory baselines. ⛔ **But the feedforward-NN floor dominates absolutely in every cell (−3.5…−42.2 pp)** ⇒ **N90**, and CM-23 splits (shape survives, dominance retracted). **What remains `OPEN` and is now the sharpest version of this entry:** *a protocol where the query does NOT live in a space the store is metric-native to* (cross-modal, or a φ-latent query against a raw-payload store) — the engineer's own diagnosis of where the NN floor should stop being at ceiling and the mechanism claim would gain teeth. Also open: multi-seed error bars, CIFAR, and the **mask-vs-noise gap as a theory lead** (the boost recovers structured erasure far better than full-field noise — worth a theory pass, not just a null).
- **⭐ A benchmark where the capacity edge is the headline metric — and where CLU can beat ATTENTION, not just the GRU.** *What we haven't shown:* any external task where graceful capacity under item load (the one surviving edge: crossover kv≈4, 2.1× at kv=16 vs a matched, rescued, gated GRU — N78) is what the leaderboard scores. *Why it matters:* against attention CLU currently loses everywhere (0.856 at kv=16); the edge is real but has no venue. ⚠ Constraint discovered at w21: MQAR itself is **structurally unwinnable** for any fixed-state recurrence (Ω(N), Arora Thm 3.1) — the task must be one where a fixed state is the *point* (streaming/online, memory-bounded regimes). Status: `OPEN`.
- **⭐ The dimension-aware-budget re-run — pins the primitive exponent (cheap, decisive).** *What we haven't shown:* the learned-capacity law with the write given a budget that scales with the address **dimension** (`min_atoms ∝ c^d`), so d=8 is no longer starved. *Why it matters:* converts N82's "probably geometry" into **"geometry, proven"** — the clean scaling law referees want; today the exponent is unpinned (base 1.2–1.7) and the d=8 point is an optimizer failure. Status: **`→ SHOWN (wave-23, dimension-aware-budget)` — the law is PINNED: `K_learned(d) = min(2^d, K_ceiling≈32)`.** Base-2 doubling per dimension for **d≤5** — *exactly the designed `4·2^d` rate, so "the wall is geometry" is **VINDICATED for d≤5*** — then a **d-INDEPENDENT WRITE ceiling at ~32** for d≥5. The w22 **d=8 stall is resolved (8→32, monotonicity restored)** and **H-LEARNING-at-8 is rejected**; the designed/learned tax **stops widening** (plateaus ~1/8 for d≥5). ⛔ The raw-sweep base **√2 (1.44) must never be quoted as the exponent** (**N92**). ⚠ Residual `OPEN` items: the d=4/d=5 walls rest on **2-seed** re-checks (the write is seed-fragile at the 0.9 rung) — a 3-seed re-run at 2× budget is the tidy confirmation; and the designed line is **censored at k_cap 256** for d≥5, so the theoretical `4·2^d` ceiling at d=8 (1024) is unverified.
- **The learned read-in `φ` BEFORE any new benchmark.** *Cross-ref, not duplicated:* the I/O-gap entry above is now the **declared bottleneck** (Hub, w22: *"every 'CLU on raw data' loss traces to feeding raw inputs into valleys with no learned embedding; nothing downstream is fair until it exists"*). The Hopfield result is the cleanest demonstration yet: CLU on raw pixels ties the closed-form Hopfield line while a metric-native trivial baseline wins — "attention on raw pixels with no embedding layer." Status: **`→ SHOWN (wave-23, phi-read-in)` — and the bottleneck did NOT explain the losses.** `φ` is built (two independent arms, both fair by construction); it is worth a *huge* lift over raw-space CLU (CIFAR chance → 0.81–0.97), and **the store still does not beat kNN-in-φ on any axis (N89)**. ⇒ **the "unfair without an embedding" defence is retired**, and the standing rule that replaces it: **every φ-space claim from here carries kNN-in-φ as a mandatory laundering control** (twice-instantiated: C17-3 `q_last`, w23 kNN-in-φ). The redirect it licenses: *the designed store's advantage is a **capability** (retention / interference / retry / abstention), not a static-recall number — a static masked-recall benchmark structurally cannot show it.*
- **A real Mamba baseline.** *What we haven't shown:* any CLU-vs-SSM comparison against an actual Mamba — the shipped SSM is **Mamba-lite** (no short conv, no input-dependent B/C — exactly the Zoology recall ingredients). *Why it matters:* every SSM cell in N69/N78 carries this asterisk; no published SSM claim is licensed until it is fixed. Status: `OPEN`.
- **The K=8-wall discriminator at a 0.87 bar / threshold-sensitivity sweep.** *What we haven't shown:* whether the clean curve's dips (d=4 K=16 at 0.876; d=8 K=16 at 0.887, both <0.025 under the 0.9 bar) are real — at a 0.87 bar the curve reads 4,8,16,32,16 (clean monotone, base ≈1.7). Cheap adjunct to the budget re-run. Status: `OPEN`.
- **E3 (spurious-attractor failure split) — still un-run, and the data exists.** *Carried from w20:* split the learned-rung failures by cause (`free_mlp` carries the `0.05‖q‖²` confinement that manufactures the origin trap, N71). Status: `OPEN` (cheap; was "the cheap owed measurement" two waves ago).
- **Discharged this arc — WAVE-23 additions (recorded so nobody re-runs them):** the **dimension-aware-budget re-run** (law pinned, `min(2^d, ~32)` — N92) · the **retry accuracy-vs-compute curve with all five controls** (curve real, mechanism survives, NN floor dominates — N90) · the **learned read-in `φ` + the laundering control** (φ built, premise validated, store still laundered — N89) · the **MVC-0 controller + the N75 rematch** (built; two-part verdict — N91) · the **three open reconciliations R19-1 / R19-2 / R20-1** (all resolved; two were under-trained artifacts — N94) · the **continual-learning target scan** (target picked, winnability audited — see the new section below).
- **Discharged this arc (recorded so nobody re-runs them):** the **γ sweep** (w21, N73 — γ is not the limiter; retire from open-questions lists) · the **multiplicative-gate pre-registered follow-up** (w22, `gated-write-performance` — levelling confirmed) · the **kv-sweep-at-γ=0-with-rescue re-check** (w22 §3 — crossover kv≈4, margin 2.1×) · the **modern-Hopfield protocol benchmark** (w22, N81 — run; verdict: bad home for capacity, keep the retry/retention capabilities) · the **admission-gate-vs-w20-interference test** (w21, N74 — the gate cannot fire; the write operator is the lever).

## ⭐ Wave-23 fold — the write ceiling, φ-space capabilities, and the continual-learning entry (added 2026-07-24 by `doc-curator-w23-sync`)

> **Context.** Wave-23 built the three things the program said it was missing (a learned `φ`, a retry curve with controls, a controller) and pinned the capacity law. **The recurring shape across all three tasks: a trivial or strong baseline dominates the absolute headline number, while CLU owns a mechanism/capability axis those baselines structurally lack** (N89/N90/N91). Everything below is the *not-yet-attempted* half of that position. ⚠ Standing scope guard: nothing here is emergence — the store, the wells and the controller are **designed**; only `φ` is learned, and it is learned **off** the CLU side.

- **⭐⭐ BREAKING THE WRITE CEILING — the single highest-value next experiment named by this wave.** **Status update (wave-24): `TASKED(write-ceiling-break)`** — commissioned as the gate on **R2**, and scoped to carry **two additions beyond this entry's original text**: (a) the **Head's scale-invariance ablation** (rescale well depths/margins so the per-item write signal is size-independent) — the *"not numerics"* clause of the Head's atoms record is **argued, not measured**, and this closes it by measurement; (b) an **objective-side arm (crowding-aware margin)**, because the write loss hits **zero while retrieval fails** ⇒ the ceiling may belong to the write *objective* rather than the write *operator*. *What we haven't shown:* whether a **masked / curriculum / sequential** write breaks the **d-independent `K_ceiling ≈ 32`**. *Why it matters:* the ceiling is now the primitive's binding capacity constraint above d=5, and it is **provably not geometry** (designed reaches ≥256) and **not parameter count** (d=6 K=64 gets **worse** with 2× atoms: 0.855 → 0.809 at 36.9k → 73.7k params) — it is *the static GLOBAL write's ability to dig many disjoint wells jointly*. The lever is already indicated by the program's most robust mechanistic result: **masked writes are bit-local at any d (8474× at d=2, 3434× at d=4; the w22 amplification 197×→599×→6332×)**. If a masked/curriculum write lifts K=64 at d=6, the capacity law becomes `2^d` over a much longer range and the *designed/learned tax* collapses; if it does not, the ceiling is a property of the write **objective**, which is a sharper and still-publishable negative. *Where it lands:* the memory/performance paper's capacity section; gates every "learned capacity" sentence. Status: ~~`OPEN`~~ **→ SHOWN across w24–w25 — the question is fully answered, and the answer changed what the ceiling IS.** w24: **no write operator raises it** (masked/sequential backfire; scale-invariant and crowding-aware fail; N96). w25: **the ceiling is not the write operator at all** — it is a **read-out reach condition** (N100), and the w23 walls sit on the excursion's far side. ⇒ the successor question is **not** another write objective but the **read-out interface** (the w25 fold's first item).
- **⭐ The φ-space retry extension — now GREEN, and unattempted.** **Status update (wave-24): partially absorbed into `TASKED(headroom-retry-benchmark)`** — the w24 task is scoped on **ambiguity regimes** (structured/correlated occlusion · crowded store · partial keys), of which *"a query the store is not metric-native to"* (this entry) is one instance; ⚠ **the explicit φ-space composition is not itself named in that task file, so it remains `OPEN` unless the engineer folds it in.** *What we haven't shown:* the retry ladder run **in φ-space** rather than on raw pixels. *Why it matters:* the gate transfers — **confidence (distance-to-nearest-well at settle) separates correct from incorrect first-pass reads in φ-space at AUROC 0.845–0.988** across all four (dataset×arm) cells (pre-registered ≥0.65; incorrect reads settle 1.6–8.7× farther) — and φ-space is the closest available instance of *"a query the store is not metric-native to,"* which is exactly where N90's NN floor should stop being at ceiling. **The two threads were run in separate worktrees and never composed.** *Where it lands:* the retry/test-time-compute story; the RUD-C spec already accommodates it (swap `read`/`retry` callables). Status: ~~`OPEN`~~ **→ SHOWN (wave-25, `cl-entry-build` Item 3 → N103) — the composition happened inside the CL entry, and it produced the program's first non-losing anytime read (a TIE, +0.8 ± 1.6 pp over the matched-compute φ-floor, 6 seeds; mechanism +4.6 ± 2.2 pp over random kick, 6/6).** ⚠ The gate transfers but **τ does not bind in φ-space** (1 seed — multi-seed owed), and a **second native regime is still needed** before the tie is more than a lead (see the w25 fold).
- **⭐ The continual-learning entry (Phase-2 candidate) — target picked, never built.** **Status update (wave-24): target RATIFIED (Head+Advisor, Part 2 point 5) and the entry is DEFERRED TO w25**; its blocker is commissioned as `TASKED(phi-stream-discipline)`. ⚠ **Head ruling that now binds the entry: winning the replay-free class while sitting BELOW replay IS a publishable success** — the replay-handicapped regimes are the *strengthening follow-up, not a rescue* (see the w24 fold below). *What we haven't shown:* CLU on **any** standard CL protocol. Recommended by `continual-learning-recon`: **PRIMARY = rehearsal-free Class-IL, Split-MNIST → Split-CIFAR-10, from scratch**, reported under **van de Ven's three-scenario taxonomy** with **ACC + forgetting/BWT (formulas pinned to GEM, arXiv:1706.08840)**; **FALLBACK = strict online single-pass Class-IL** (average online accuracy, SQHN's home turf). Entry = **designed store + learned `φ` + the MVC-0 controller**, replay-free, differentiated by per-item decay and the retry read. *Why it matters:* forgetting/BWT is *natively* what the designed store already measures (gated retention **1.000 vs 0.16**), the weight class is laptop/CSF3, and the episodic-memory-module slot is refereed and fair (iCaRL, SQHN, FearNet, MbPA). ⛔ **Binding scope caveat that must travel with any CL claim:** *Class-IL at small scale is **solved by replay** — DGR **90.79%** / iCaRL **94.57%** on Split-MNIST Class-IL vs EWC/SI ≈ **20%** (≈chance) — and even by a dumb balanced buffer (**GDumb**). CLU's winnable claim is "best replay-FREE method + per-item retention control + an adaptive-compute retry read," **NOT** "beats replay."* **Mandatory baselines or the entry is "solved by a buffer": tuned ER + iCaRL + GDumb at matched memory (N78 discipline), plus EWC/SI as the known null.** ⚠ **Weight-class fork, decide before building:** the modern Split-CIFAR-100/ImageNet-R Class-IL SOTA (~86–88% / ~69–72%) is **prompt-tuning on a pretrained ViT** — incomparable to a from-scratch entry; cite as related work, do not enter. Status: ~~`OPEN`~~ **→ SHOWN (wave-25) — the entry EXISTS, wins the rehearsal-free class on MNIST, is LAUNDERED, and is a NULL on CIFAR.**
  - > **→ SHOWN (wave-25, `cl-entry-build` → N101/N102/N103): built end-to-end (`chlu exp-cl-entry`), 3 seeds, matched 200-item memory, zero gradient steps on the stream.** **Split-MNIST Class-IL: 0.707 ± 0.012 / BWT −0.169 — best rehearsal-free method, +0.510 over EWC (0.196)**, *in a class whose incumbents fail by construction (our harness reproduces their published values to ≤0.5 pp — the calibration check)*; **−0.153 below iCaRL** (a CM-23(n) publishable success); ⛔ **laundered by a kNN-in-φ ring buffer at matched memory (−0.036)** — the 4th consecutive wave. ⭐ **The store's own axis is forgetting: BWT −0.169 vs −0.99, and better than tuned ER at matched items (0.169 vs 0.264) with 24.5× fewer floats.** ⛔ **Split-CIFAR-10 is a NULL** (0.149, below LwF) — the **feature space**, not the discipline. ⚠ **Head ruling (Addendum 2, 2026-07-28): NOT "an external benchmark won" for the score sentence — supplementary only, two-clause form.** ⚠ **LwF is the one baseline not reproduced (19.6 vs 23.9) and must be re-tuned before the table is published** ⇒ a NEW OPEN item. ⇒ **the strengthening follow-ups below (replay-handicapped regimes) are unchanged and still OPEN; the new frontier is the forgetting-vs-BYTES frontier.**
- **The address-space sizing rule — the CL entry's first design decision, and it is forced.** *What we haven't shown:* a CL benchmark run on a **sized** CLU store. *Why it matters:* **N91 is a design constraint, not just a negative** — on a *fixed* small space the controller's per-offered is capped at `N_pack/K` (0.081 at K=64, last of seven); on a **sized** space (`R = 0.808·√K`) it reaches **0.669 and beats all four primitives**. ⇒ any CL entry must either **(a) size the address space to the task's item count** (packing bound ≥ K) or **(b) accept abstention and be scored per-admitted with the admitted fraction reported.** Untested sub-item: **farthest-point (rather than uniform-random) proposal sampling** to close the achieved-packing gap **42.8 → 64** — *the cheapest lever to make CLU+controller dominate per-offered cleanly* (`d_eff<d` shell concentration). Status: `OPEN` (cheap).
- **Prior-art deltas that gate the CL and retry novelty claims (reviewer-facing, unfunded).** **(i) SQHN (Nature Communications 2024, doi:10.1038/s41467-024-46976-4)** already occupies *"energy-based Hopfield-class store, online-continual, replay-free, parameter-isolation"* — **do not re-derive its claim and call it new**; CLU's enumerated delta is a **continuous** designed landscape + a **fiber payload channel separate from the address** + **per-item decay/eviction schedules** + **relaxation/retry read** + **learned `φ`** (SQHN is discrete/quantized one-hot with hard parameter isolation). **(ii) The retry benchmark does NOT collide** with the mainstream test-time-compute-for-retrieval line (RAG/LLM-external-KB — a different setting); its two genuine neighbours are **Energy-Based Transformers (arXiv:2507.02092)** — the framing-level incumbent for energy-as-verifier test-time compute — and **modern-Hopfield multi-step retrieval** (= the `hopfield_ksteps` control, already implemented) ⇒ **no spec change; frame as associative-memory read-refinement / anytime retrieval, never "test-time RAG," and cite both.** ⚠ Verification debts carried: SQHN's exact arXiv id, EBT's author list, the CIFAR-100/ImageNet-R prompt-SOTA figures (all single-sourced). Status: `OPEN` (cite-and-distinguish debts; blocking on wording, not on science).
- **Whether any *continuous* (non-quantized) energy/Hamiltonian store has ever been run on standard Split-MNIST Class-IL.** *Why it matters:* **a negative here strengthens the novelty claim materially** and is a single citation-graph sweep; it is the CL analogue of the outstanding *"no Hamiltonian addressable memory exists"* sweep. Status: `OPEN` (scout-sized).
- **Multi-seed error bars on the whole w23 frontier.** Three of four engineer results are **seed 0 single-seed** (φ, retry) or **2-seed at the frontier cells** (budget adequacy). The mechanisms are large-margin; the digits are not. *Why it matters:* every one of these numbers is a candidate paper table. Status: **`TASKED(multi-seed-w23)` (wave-24)** — Head ruling: *multi-seed before any paper number*; the task prioritises the **laundering verdict** and the **kick/ensemble flatness** as the two tier-A load-bearing results if compute binds.

## ⭐ Wave-24 fold — the four dials, the R1–R5 result set, and the boundaries the direction ruling created (added 2026-07-24 by `doc-curator-w24-plainlang`)

> **Context.** The Head+Advisor direction ruling (`head-advisor-w23-direction.md`, 2026-07-24; binding) reframed the thesis: **the store's value is control over memory in time — admission · lifetimes · isolation under sequential writes · compute-adaptive reads — not static lookup**, and every future claim should be a claim about one of those four dials. That reframe *creates* scope boundaries: **each dial now needs a law demonstrated at a scale and in a regime we have not reached**, and the five named results (R1–R5) are each one unshown thing. Everything below is *not-yet-attempted*; the tried-and-failed half is `negative_results.md` (N89–N94). ⚠ Standing scope guard unchanged: the store, the wells and the controller are **designed**; only `φ` is learned, and off the CLU side. **Roadmap `v0.7` is the plan of record for these.**

- **⭐⭐ R1 — "CERTIFIED" per-item lifetimes, against the unlearning literature's own benchmarks.** *What we haven't shown:* that per-item decay/deletion is **certified in the sense machine-unlearning referees mean** (an exactness or indistinguishability guarantee), and that it survives contact with that field's benchmarks and threat models. *Why it matters:* R1 is the result the Head calls *closest to done* — the machinery exists (`controller-mvp`: exact `exp(−leak·t)`, half-life 1.98 measured, permanent+leaky in one store, self-eviction) — **but "mostly framing" is an assumption, not a finding.** ⚠ **Two named risks the scout is testing:** (i) *"certified"* may formally require something we do not have; (ii) **"deletion by construction" may already be owned** — a kNN datastore also deletes exactly, which is the laundering control's shape applied to the unlearning claim. *Where it lands:* R1 / Phase 3; ICLR + NMI. Status: **`TASKED(unlearning-recon)` (w24, scoping only — the packaging itself is unfunded)**.
  - > **→ SHOWN (scoping) (wave-24, `unlearning-recon`) — and the answer is NO, "mostly framing" was wrong: R1 is one forbidden word plus one unfixed technical gap.** (i) *"certified"* formally requires (ε,δ)-indistinguishability from retraining (Guo et al. ICML 2020, Def 1–2) — CLU satisfies **none** of the four requirements ⇒ delete the word (**CM-22(m)**). (ii) "deletion by construction" is **occupied** (SILO ICLR'24, SISA, Ticketed L-U, PALL ICLR'25 — **CM-22(n)**). (iii) ⛔ **a NEW blocking technical gap: the MVC-0 controller's refuse-and-relocate + LRU eviction make the store history-dependent ⇒ CLU cannot claim even EXACT deletion** without an order-independent placement rule (**N99 / CM-22(o)**). **Approved reframe:** file R1 as a *memory-control* result — "scheduled per-item retention / set-at-write-time lifetimes with an exact decay law" — with unlearning cited as motivation and an explicit "no (ε,δ) claim" sentence. Two new OPEN items spun out below (order-independent placement; partially-decayed distinguishability). Naming: never "unlearning" (Hopfield/Feinstein/Palmer 1983). Status of the *reframe*: `→ SHOWN`; the *packaging as a paper section* remains `OPEN`.
- **⭐⭐ R2 at d=16+ — the capacity law beyond the write ceiling, at O(1) read cost and matched parameters.** *What we haven't shown:* anything above **d=8 / K=32 learned**; the designed line is **censored at `k_cap` 256**, so even the designed `4·2^d = 1024` at d=8 is unverified. *Why it matters:* the screenshot-able log-linear figure and the NMI law both live here, and the law is currently *pinned only inside the region the ceiling allows*. ⛔ **Boundary that must travel (Head ruling, Hub-confirmed): R2 is EXEMPT from the masked-recall demotion because it is a law about the primitive, not a competitive claim — and its figure must NEVER be framed as beating anything.** Carry the denominators (capacity-per-parameter is a comparison we lose, **N77**). Status: `OPEN` (**gated on `write-ceiling-break`; Phase 3**).
  - > **Update (wave-24, `write-ceiling-break` → N96): the write-lever route to R2 is CLOSED, but a geometric route just OPENED (and R2 at d=16+ is still censored).** No write operator (masked/sequential — which backfires; scale-invariant; crowding-aware; combo) raises `K_ceiling≈32` at d∈{4,6,8}; scale-dilution ruled out by measurement (|Δ|=0.010). ⚠ **The mechanism attribution is CONTESTED (Hub ruling, N92/N96): "the ceiling is the write operator" is NOT settled** — `lattice-capacity-theory` §4.2 offers a geometric fixed-width-lock account (`sep_crit ≈ 2.4–3.0×well width` at every wall d=2…8) that predicts the d-independence AND a one-flag route to R2 that needs no lattice: **`atom_init_width 0.30 → 0.15` (matching the designed width at the query-noise floor), predicted to move `K_learned` by ~`2^d`** (lattice §5.2). ⇒ **the decisive free check is the trained-`log_width` dump (lattice §5.0); it is now the highest-value R2 experiment**, ahead of any lattice engineering. R2 at d=16+ remains `OPEN` (designed line censored at 256).
  - > **Update (wave-25, `r2-geometry-revival` → N100): the geometric route is now CLOSED TOO — and the ceiling has a third, better explanation.** The free check ran and **passed**; the one-flag experiment it authorised **failed in the opposite direction** (`K_learned(4)` drops **below 16**: 0.9368 ± 0.0133 → 0.5944 ± 0.0164, 3 seeds, 28 SE) ⇒ ⛔ **both the write-operator and the width-lock attributions are retired as causes; `lattice §5.2`'s `×2^d` prediction is dead.** The measured constraint is a **read-out REACH condition** (`s ≳ σ_q` below, `s ≳ |a|_max/κ`, κ ≈ 3, above), and **the w23 walls sit on the excursion's far side** (half excursion: d=4 K=32 **0.824 → 1.000**, K=64 → **0.9922**, at both widths) ⚠ *on a noiseless payload channel, so mechanism-only — no `K_learned` at pscale ≠ 1.* ⇒ **R2's surviving route is the READ-OUT EXCURSION** (the new OPEN item in the w25 fold below), and **R2 at d=16+ remains `OPEN`** with the designed line still censored at 256. ⚠ Also: **d=8 K=64 is no longer a wall** (0.9121 ± 0.0029), and sharding fixes d=6 K=64 but does **not** unclamp `K = 32N` (N105/N106).
- **⭐⭐ The lattice question — does sharding across N units multiply capacity (~32·N) WITHOUT optimizer synchronization?** *What we haven't shown:* that the ~32 write ceiling is *per-unit* rather than global. **The Head's hypothesis, advisor-refined:** capacity multiplies **iff writes are local** — masked writes share no parameters across units, so there is nothing to sync; the only global object is **routing**, which is the controller's placement verb, not an optimizer. ⇒ the Head's original *"chain optimizers / map disconnected optimizer spaces"* concern **dissolves under write-locality**. *Why it matters:* it is the only route on the table from `K ≈ 32` to a headline capacity number without breaking the write, and it converts existing machinery (`CLULattice`, wormholes) into a capacity mechanism. ⚠ **Unshown sub-questions the theory pass must name: the read cost of N units** (does retrieval stay O(1), or become O(N) routing?), **the failure modes of locality** (what shares parameters in practice), and **a falsifiable w25 prediction**. Status: **`TASKED(lattice-capacity-theory)` (w24, theory only — no experiment funded)**.
  - > **→ SHOWN (theory) (wave-24, `lattice-capacity-theory`) — the Head's worry has no referent, but sharding cannot exceed geometry.** **Prop L2 (proven + measured):** AdamW's `(m,v)` state factorizes over parameter blocks, so a masked write on disjoint atom groups **IS N independent optimizers** (joint-masked ≡ N-independent to the site tail) — *"chaining optimizers / mapping disconnected optimizer spaces" has no referent.* **But `K_total = min(K_addr(geometry), N·K_ceiling)`** — sharding RELOCATES the binding constraint onto geometry and can NEVER exceed it, and it REQUIRES a **global address allocator** (a registry, not an optimizer). **Read-cost verdict:** the shipped mixture store (`n_groups`) is **O(1) in N exactly** (grouping is invisible to the physics); a product store is O(1) in rollout depth and O(N) in pointwise scores (~N/2400 of one read ⇒ free to N≈100). ⛔ **Never route on post-settle energy** (N97); route on pre-settle energy (R2) or settling displacement (R3). ⚠ The routing half is **classical nearest-neighbour indexing** — never present capacity-by-sharding as a dynamical result (N89 discipline). The theory half is `→ SHOWN`; the **experiment** (the §5.1 2×2 sharding capacity test) is the new OPEN item below.
- **⭐ R3's headroom regime — a benchmark where all methods land ~0.6–0.7 and the boost buys a visible margin at stated extra compute.** *What we haven't shown:* the retry curve on **any** task with headroom; every measured cell had a saturated feedforward floor (0.99–1.00) that structurally cannot be beaten (**N90**). ⚠ **Binding design constraint from the w23 physics (Head + advisor): the failure mode must be AMBIGUITY — structured/correlated occlusion, well-crowding, partial keys — NOT destruction (Gaussian noise), which the boost cannot recover past the basin cliff.** ⭐ *Crowded-store retrieval inside the CL entry supplies this regime for free* — an untested and cheap composition. Status: **`TASKED(headroom-retry-benchmark)` (w24)**; the CL-crowding composition remains `OPEN`.
  - > **→ SHOWN (wave-24, `headroom-retry-benchmark`) — headroom WAS built from ambiguity, and the leaderboard answer is still a decision-grade NO (N95).** Two ambiguity regimes (block occlusion + contracted crowded store, 3 seeds, first-pass 0.26–0.93 off ceiling) — yet CLU-gated retry does NOT beat the ML-optimal `feedforward_nn_masked` erasure oracle (=1.000) in any of 8 cells (−3.5…−38 pp); **cannot be blamed on saturation.** The mechanism half survives (kick/ensemble flat-or-declining; boost +2.3…+36.2 pp; auto-stops ×1.20–1.81). ⭐ **The one untested route to a leaderboard win survives as a NEW OPEN boundary (below): a query in a space the store is NOT metric-native to (cross-modal / φ-latent), where the masked oracle stops being trivially 1.000.** RUD-C: mandatory baseline #1 = the masked observed-dims oracle. The CL-crowding composition remains `OPEN`.
- **⭐ R4's replay-handicapped regimes — four unprobed regimes, one of which makes replay ILLEGAL.** *What we haven't shown:* CL under (i) **strict single-pass online** (replay structurally limited), (ii) **tiny memory budgets** (buffers dilute), (iii) **long task sequences, 20–50 tasks**, (iv) ⭐ **privacy-constrained CL where raw exemplars are disallowed by rule — replay is illegal and a landscape store is the only legal entrant.** *Why it matters:* these are where a replay-free method's structural advantage becomes a *rule*, not a margin; (iv) is the strongest available framing for "no raw data is ever stored". ⚠ **Head filing ruling that scopes them: they are the STRENGTHENING FOLLOW-UP, not a rescue** — winning the replay-free class while sitting below replay is already a publishable success, and the success bar is *win the replay-free class across a suite, and approach — if not equal/beat — replay at matched memory*. Status: `OPEN` (**Phase 4; do not use them to justify a weak Phase-2 result**).
- **⭐ R5 — the drop-in `CLULayer`: a bounded-memory long-context store for transformer stacks (a KV-cache that does not grow, with retention dials).** *What we haven't shown:* CLU inside a transformer stack at all, at any scale, on any long-context task. *Why it matters:* **it is the adoption hook** — the result that converts readers into users and users into kernel-writers, which is the program's stated ambition (the transformer's growth mechanism, on purpose). ⚠ **Named as the RISKIEST of the five by the Head:** w22's sequence-slot result was **levelling, not winning**, and the pitch is deliberately a *constraint* pitch (bounded memory + dials), never a supremacy pitch. Prerequisites not held today: R1/R3's dials working under a *learned* landscape, and CSF3-scale training. Status: `OPEN` (**Phase 4; unfunded**).
- **The φ-stream discipline as a scientific boundary, not just a leakage guard.** *What we haven't shown:* how much a **strictly legal** φ costs as tasks accumulate. In Class-IL, φ may **not** be trained on data from future tasks (the w23 `phi-read-in` disjoint pool saw all classes — fine there, **leakage here**). Head ruling: **task-1-only φ = PRIMARY; generic-frozen = a declared upper bound, never the headline; online φ = later, as its own experiment.** ⚠ *The unshown quantity is the **cost-of-strictness curve** — the gap between task-1-only and generic-frozen as the task index grows — which is a scientific result in its own right and one no CL paper we cite reports.* Status: **`TASKED(phi-stream-discipline)` (w24; blocks w25)**.
  - > **→ SHOWN (wave-24, `phi-stream-discipline`) — the cost-of-strictness curve is measured, and it is ≈0 at the shipped feature dimension.** End-of-stream (Split-MNIST 5×2, M=160, 3 seeds): task1_only vs generic-frozen identity gap **−0.023 (PCA-32) / +0.006 (AE-32)**, ≈0 class — **task1_only φ RATIFIED as primary; w25's CL entry does NOT need online φ.** The cost is a *tilt* across task index (+0.034…+0.077/task), not a growing deficit — a narrow φ **out-resolves** a broad φ on its own dense classes (gap −0.115 at task 0). ⚠ **New binding protocol parameter: `phi_dim ≥ 16` for any strict-φ claim** — the null is capacity slack, not absence of cost (gap +0.117/+0.190 at `phi_dim=4`; the strict basis captures 10–18 pp less later-task variance at every dim). N89 laundering fires in all 16 stress cells; the store-advantage-under-strictness watch-item does NOT fire. ⚠ **Everything here is MNIST — Split-CIFAR-10 (where a task-1-only φ should actually bite) is a NEW OPEN item below.**
- **The "one slide" claim itself — legibility as an adoption hypothesis.** *What we haven't shown:* that *write = dig a valley · read = drop a ball · forget = let it fill in* survives contact with readers who did not build it — i.e. that a third party can reimplement the primitive from the picture plus the module. *Why it matters:* the ambition is adoption, and the transformer's adoption rested on being **parallelizable, general, simple to reimplement**; CLU is currently **none of the first two at scale** and the third is untested. ⚠ Related unshown item the Head has *chosen* rather than deferred: the **deliberate *"inefficiencies we leave to you"* section** in the open release — a positioning device with no precedent in this program. Status: `OPEN` (**not a measurement; a release-design boundary, recorded so it is not discovered at submission time**).
- **Differentiability and training speed — still declared debt, still unshown.** *Carried forward, restated because the reframe does not discharge it:* the Head's standing scope ruling is that **differentiability and training efficiency are NOT first-paper requirements** (*"if we have good performance, the community will work with us to make it efficient"*), **but the debt must be STATED, not discovered later.** ⚠ The four dials sharpen it: three of the four (admission, lifetimes, isolation) are today **hand-coded policies on a designed store**, so *"is any of this trainable end-to-end?"* is the first question an adopter asks. Status: `OPEN`/`PARKED(Head ruling — state it, don't fund it yet)`.

### ⭐ Wave-24 experimental fold (part 2) — the boundaries the w24 outputs surfaced (added 2026-07-25 by `doc-curator-w24-sync-part2`)

> **Context.** These are the *new not-yet-attempted* things the eight w24 outputs created (distinct from the tried-and-failed half, `negative_results.md` N95–N99). The single most consequential is the geometric-vs-operator adjudication of the K≈32 ceiling, whose decisive check is free.

- **⭐⭐ The geometric width-lock account of the K≈32 ceiling — a free, decisive check and a one-flag route to R2 that needs no lattice.** *What we haven't shown:* whether the ceiling is an **operator** limit (write-ceiling's reading) or a **geometric fixed-width lock** (`lattice §4.2`: `sep_crit ≈ 2.4–3.0 × well width` at every designed AND learned wall d=2…8, reproducing the tax as `2^{-d}` from `well_width 0.15` vs `atom_init_width 0.30`). *Why it matters:* the two accounts predict **opposite outcomes** for R2, and one of them (geometric) hands R2 a one-flag experiment. ⚠ **Mechanism CONTESTED (Hub ruling) — do not word N92/N96 as settled either way.** *Where it lands:* **§5.0 — dump the trained `log_width` on the w23 `dimension-aware-budget` checkpoints** (≤1 hour, analyst/engineer; prediction: median effective width ≥ 0.28, ratio `minsep/width ∈ [2.4,3.1]` at every d; falsifier: widths trained below ~0.18 ⇒ §4.2 dead, operator reading stands). **§5.2 — `atom_init_width 0.30 → 0.15` at ≥3 seeds under adequate budget** (predicted `K_learned(4)` 16→64–128, `K_learned(6)` 32→≥128, ×`2^d`). Status: ~~`THEORY-ONLY`~~ **→ SHOWN (wave-25, `r2-geometry-revival`) — and BOTH accounts lost.**
  - > **→ SHOWN (wave-25, `r2-geometry-revival` → N100): the free check ran, it authorised the one-flag experiment, and the experiment refuted the account that authorised it — the ceiling is a THIRD thing.** §5.0's prediction **held** (trained widths 0.215–0.364, never below the 0.18 falsifier; ratio range 1.36×; **a perfect d ≥ 4 classifier**, boundary 2.30–2.49, including the out-of-sample d=8 K=64 crack cell). §5.2's prediction **failed in the opposite direction**: `atom_init_width 0.30 → 0.15` moves `K_learned(4)` **below 16** (0.9368 ± 0.0133 → **0.5944 ± 0.0164**, 3 seeds, 28 SE, flat at 2× atoms) ⇒ ⛔ **the width route to R2 is CLOSED and `lattice §5.2` is REFUTED BY EXPERIMENT.** The binding constraint is a **read-out REACH condition** — `s ≳ σ_q` below, **`s ≳ |a|_max/κ` (κ ≈ 3)** above — measured per-item (corr −0.887 with `|a_i|`; force at the launch manifold collapses 5.1×) and **confirmed by intervention** (half excursion: d=4 K=32 **0.824 → 1.000**, d=4 K=64 → **0.9922**, at both widths). ⛔ **Both prior attributions retired as causes**; `sep/width` survives as a **correlate**. ⚠ **No `K_learned` at `pscale ≠ 1`.** ⇒ **two NEW OPEN items below** (the fair excursion experiment; the joint init × width factorial).
- **⭐ The lattice §5.1 2×2 sharding capacity experiment — the actual test of `K_total = min(K_addr, N·K_ceiling)`.** *What we haven't shown:* that the ~32 ceiling is per-unit rather than global. *The discriminator is the 2×2, not any single cell:* d=6 K=64 as 2 shards × 32 should PASS (additivity), while d=4 K=32 as 2×16 should still FAIL (it is `K_addr`-bound, below the ceiling) — both fixed ⇒ ceiling was entirely per-dig (`K=32N`), only d=6 fixed ⇒ Theorem L1, neither fixed ⇒ **additivity REFUTED**. Requires global farthest-point allocation over the union + the localized atom init (N98) + per-shard query noise. Status: ~~`OPEN`~~ **→ SHOWN (wave-25, `lattice-sharded-store`).**
  - > **→ SHOWN (wave-25, `lattice-sharded-store` → N104–N107): the 2×2 ran and the discriminator answered "Theorem L1", not `K = 32N`.** **d=6 K=64 IS fixed by sharding** — 0.9251 ± 0.0030 (shipped dynamical router + the N98 init) vs the monolithic laundering line **0.8802 ± 0.0075 at identical init and identical total atoms**; 0.9043 ± 0.0064 vs 0.8288 ± 0.0186 with a classical registry router and no init change. **d=4 K=32 is NOT fixed even with routing removed in both arms** (0.8932 / 0.8984 — quote as "0.89–0.90, does not clear 0.90") ⇒ **`K_total = min(K_addr, Σ_r K_write)`; `K = 32N` is NOT unclamped.** ⭐ Interpretable half: **~60 % of the d=6 monolithic deficit is ROUTING, not writing**; the write is **literally additive** (separate parameter trees, zero optimizer sync) and **4–5× cheaper wall-clock**. ⛔ Two new guards: **the atom floor is per-STORE** (matched N-way splits are automatically N× starved ⇒ wins conservative, N ≥ 4 collapses are **not** capacity results — N107) and ⛔ **R3 routing is BROKEN on the shipped flat-vacuum geometry** (1.000/0.549/0.236/0.228; only R2 + the classical registry survive — N104 extends N97). Also: **d=8 K=64 is no longer a wall** (monolithic 0.9121 ± 0.0029). **Still OPEN:** the budget-adequate d=8 arms and the d=8 K=256 8×32 probe (compute), and a 5-seed re-check of the d=4 K=32 RG band.
- **Localized atom initialisation for `AtomDictionaryPotential` groups (the N98 fix).** *What we haven't shown:* that a masked write is bit-independent across shards. The measured 1.4% amp deviation (3 orders above the site tail) is an **init defect** (`init_scale=1.0` scatters every group's atoms), not physics; the ~5-line fix (init group *j*'s atoms near site *j*) makes Prop L2 exact in practice. Prerequisite for any honest sharding claim. Status: ~~`OPEN`~~ **→ SHOWN (wave-25, built + measured; ⛔ NOT promoted to a default).**
  - > **→ SHOWN (wave-25, `lattice-sharded-store` §5 → N98 w25 block): built, and it is the biggest single knob of the wave.** Applied identically to both arms, 3 seeds, d=6 K=64, radius `2s = 0.6`, **address axes only**: **+0.051 monolithic (0.8288 → 0.8802) and +0.082 sharded (0.8434 → 0.9251 PASS)**, and it **repairs the dynamical router (route accuracy 0.913 → 0.970)** and halves payload error. **Ships OFF and is bit-identical when disabled** (`jax.random.fold_in(key, 1)` leaves even the default RNG stream untouched). ⚠ Address axes only *by design*: localizing the payload axis would hand the writer the value it must learn (N46) **and** destroy the basin-reach property N100 shows is load-bearing. ⛔ **Head ruling (Addendum 2 decision 4): NOT promoted to a default yet — run the init × width 2×2 factorial with the interaction term first** (the two levers may be the same effect twice). ⇒ **NEW OPEN item below.**
- **⭐ A retrieval regime where the store is NOT metric-native to the query (cross-modal / φ-latent) — the one untested route to a leaderboard win.** *What we haven't shown:* any ambiguity benchmark where the ML-optimal masked-NN oracle stops being trivially 1.000. On erasure protocols identity retrieval under *known* erasure is information-theoretically trivial, so "ambiguity" only bites methods that ignore the mask (N95). A query living in a space the store must *settle into* to resolve is the only place CLU's dynamics could add value over a classical NN read. *Where it lands:* coordinate with the φ threads / R3 / the CL entry. Status: ~~`OPEN`~~ **→ SHOWN (wave-25) — the regime was reached, and the answer is a TIE, not a win.**
  - > **→ SHOWN (wave-25, `cl-entry-build` Item 3 → N103): the CL entry supplied the regime for free, exactly as predicted, and the anytime read stopped losing.** Corruption in **pixel space**, store addressed in **φ** ⇒ **no constructible mask oracle**. Measured at the hardest level (6 seeds): gated **0.885 ± 0.018 @ 1.40 ± 0.20×** vs matched-compute feedforward-in-φ **0.877 ± 0.025** ⇒ **+0.8 ± 1.6 pp — a TIE** (3/6 seeds positive), with the mechanism intact (**+4.6 ± 2.2 pp over random kick, 6/6**; ungated → 0.000). **First non-losing anytime read in the program's history** (w24: −3.5…−38 pp in 8/8) — and a tie **because a pre-registered re-run turned an apparent +0.7 pp win into one.** ⚠ **One dataset, one corruption level**, and on CIFAR the kNN floor is 1.000 so the cell is unwinnable there. ⇒ **NEW OPEN item below: a SECOND native regime.**
- **Order-independent placement rule for the store (the R1 exact-deletion exit).** *What we haven't shown:* a placement/eviction rule under which removing item *i* reproduces the never-written store (N99). Two exits: re-run placement on the retained set (= retraining, defeats the purpose) or **prove an order-independent rule** (candidate w25 theory task). ⚠ May already exist in the caching / streaming-algorithms literature — a scout sub-question. Without it, R1 must be scoped to "scheduled retention," never "deletion." Status: ~~`OPEN`~~ **→ SHOWN at theory + reference level (wave-25); the LANDING is a new OPEN item.**
  - > **→ SHOWN (wave-25, `order-independent-placement` → N99 w25 block): the rule exists, and its packing price is NEGATIVE.** **PGCP** (priority-greedy canonical placement — strongly-history-independent hashing transplanted to the continuous designed store): `Store(S)` **proven a set function**, **`delete = set-minus` bit-exactly**, decay/permanence **provably commute** with deletion, spacing certificate **free by construction**. Verified at **0 tolerance**: 24 exhaustive orders + 200 random orders per size + **100 write/delete interleavings** + mid-decay deletes, all bit-identical; **byte-equal on the shipped `AtomStorePotential` PyTree** (max |ΔV| = 0.0 on 64 queries) ⇒ **no production change to the store class.** Packing: **61/64 = 0.953 vs refuse-and-relocate's 42.8/64** (+42 %); churn **2.84 moves/delete at full load**. ⛔ Boundary: below capacity or set-function (priority/attribute) eviction only — **LRU is permanently excluded**. ⛔ **Three blockers before any deletion sentence ships** — the missing **`Controller.delete` verb**, **prior-art verification** (the discrete skeleton IS prior art), and the **numeric acceptance test (drive post-evict `AUC(z_hole)` 0.99985 → ~0.5)** — all three are w26 tasks. ⛔ "Certified" stays forbidden; ⛔ never quote **0.953** as measured retention until the real-read rematch.
- **Distinguishability of a PARTIALLY DECAYED item vs `leak·t` — the cheapest new measurement in the program, and a genuinely open cell.** *What we haven't shown:* what a U-LiRA / MIA adversary sees at one half-life (amp ≈ 0.06 is neither present nor absent), and what the payload retains after `evict`. The scout found **no prior work** on adversarial distinguishability of a partially-decayed memory. Pre-register a retention curve AND an MIA-style separability curve on the same axis; report where they cross. Converts a referee's hardest R1 question into a figure. Status: ~~`OPEN`~~ **→ SHOWN (wave-25) — the figure exists, and the answer is honest in both directions.**
  - > **→ SHOWN (wave-25, `mia-decay-measurement` → N108): the curves never cross — *the store stops answering before it stops leaking*.** At the last amplitude before self-eviction: **retention 0.832** vs **query-MIA AUC 0.983 (TPR 0.858 @ FPR 1 %)** vs **white-box 1.000**. ⛔ **Against an EXACT adversary decay buys nothing at any point in the item's life** (white-box AUC 1.000 at **all 18 levels**; what decays is effect size — gap **0.3935·A** to 4 dp, `d′` 1560 → 79.6) — **the falsifier fired exactly as pre-registered.** ✅ Against a **resolution-limited** adversary the graded law survives to ~2 % (`A₇₅ = 2.57 σ_obs`). ⭐ **The caveat-free differentiator is retrieval geometry:** `R₅₀` contracts **1.146 → 0.752 (1.52×)** where a TTL dict's lookup radius is a constant step at ≈0.77. ⛔ **And `evict` is not erasure:** the allocator trace gives **AUC 0.99985, TPR 1.000 @ FPR 1 %** post-evict (⚠ at 7/8 occupancy — **the occupancy sweep is a NEW OPEN item**), and the raw arrays retain address+value verbatim. ⚠ **New defect surfaced: the lifetimes dial is payload-dependent** (`r = −0.85` with `a_i²`) ⇒ **NEW OPEN item (theorist ruling first).**
- **Split-CIFAR-10 φ-stream — the rung where a task-1-only φ should actually bite.** *What we haven't shown:* that the ≈0 cost-of-strictness transfers off MNIST. CIFAR features are far less shared across classes than digit strokes; the single biggest risk to the w25 plan and cheap to test (the harness takes a `dataset` flag; only a labels branch in `load_labeled_images`). Status: ~~`OPEN`~~ **→ SHOWN (wave-25) — and the expectation was REFUTED: strictness does not bite, the feature space does.**
  - > **→ SHOWN (wave-25, `cl-entry-build` Item 5 → N102): `strict_phi_cost = +0.0011` on Split-CIFAR-10** (CLU 0.149 ± 0.013 task1_only vs 0.150 generic_frozen) ⇒ **the leakage-free φ costs nothing there either — whatever is broken is broken in both arms.** The entry is a **NULL** (below LwF 0.162) and the diagnosis is the **address space**: a 200-prototype kNN over the same PCA-32 φ reaches only **0.207–0.222**, while the store's own retrieval on those addresses is **1.000 / 0.863**. *The memory works; what it holds is uninformative about the label.* ⇒ **the entry's scope is MNIST-class data until a CIFAR-capable φ exists — a NEW OPEN item, and R4's single blocking dependency.**
- **⚠ Cite-and-differentiate debt: PALL (ICLR 2025) directly occupies the CL∩unlearning cell — add to `continual-learning-recon`'s must-cite list alongside SQHN.** *What we haven't shown:* differentiation from PALL (task-specific sparse subnetworks + episodic-memory rehearsal ⇒ exact task unlearning in a continual-learning setting) — the exact slot the w25 CL entry enters. Also mandatory for any R1/CL table: kNN row-delete · retrain-from-scratch · a TTL-dict with the same half-lives · Expire-Span; U-LiRA (not population MIA) + a benign-relearning attack for any privacy language; guardrail baselines for any LLM framing. Status: `OPEN` (positioning; blocking on multi/CL novelty wording, not the science).

### ⭐ Wave-25 fold — the boundaries the CONTACT wave created (added 2026-07-28 by `doc-curator-w25-sync`)

> **Context.** Wave-25 discharged **five** standing items on this register (the width-lock check · the 2×2 sharding experiment · the CL entry · the partially-decayed distinguishability measurement · the order-independent placement rule) and **created nine new ones**, most of them sharper than what they replaced because they now carry a *number to hit* or a *fairness condition to meet*. ⚠ **Standing scope guard unchanged:** the store, the wells and the controller are **designed**; only `φ` is learned, and off the CLU side. The tried-and-failed half is `negative_results.md` **N100–N108**. **Roadmap `v0.8` is the plan of record.**

- **⭐⭐ The FAIR excursion experiment — the one measured route left to R2, and its fairness conditions are now binding.** *What we haven't shown:* whether the reach ceiling moves when shortening the read-out excursion is **not free**. N100's intervention (0.824 → 1.000 at half excursion) was run on a **noiseless payload channel**, so it is mechanism-only. *Why it matters:* it is the **only** measured route left to moving R2 (the width route is closed), and the Head has ruled the read-out excursion **a legitimate interface parameter — an *output code*, not a difficulty knob** (Addendum 2 decision 3). *Two clean arms:* **(a) multi-channel payload** — `m` channels of excursion `|a|_max/√m` carrying the same total value precision (lowers the per-axis reach demand **without lowering information content**; the engineer's own recommendation, because it changes the *primitive's read-out geometry* and cannot be dismissed as an easier task); **(b) the annealed read** `σ_eff(t) = √(σ² + s(t)²)` (analytically free for Gaussians, **no payload-format change**). ⛔ **Five binding fairness conditions:** bits-per-item constant · byte accounting pinned · **payload noise ON (the crux)** · baselines get the same format · laundering travels. ⚠ **Known trap, stated in the source:** rescaling payloads while holding `payload_tol` fixed is **laundering**. Status: **`TASKED(r2-excursion-reach` Stage B`)` (w26)** — the wave's heaviest compute; a theorist impossibility result on read-noise would save it.
- **⭐ The joint init × width factorial (do the two w25 levers interact, or are they one effect twice?).** *What we haven't shown:* whether N98's localized init (+0.051 / +0.082) and N100's width/reach condition are **independent**. Both attack "atoms in the wrong place" from different sides; both are large. *Why it matters:* ⛔ **neither may become a default until this runs** (Head: *not yet*), and the interaction term is the whole question — Advisor-2's prior is that they are *"substantially one effect."* Status: **`TASKED(r2-excursion-reach` Stage A`)` (w26; 2×2, 3 seeds, interaction term reported).**
- **⭐⭐ A CIFAR-capable `φ` — R4's single blocking dependency, with a cheap decisive gate.** *What we haven't shown:* a feature space in which a CIFAR-class store *could* be competitive. PCA-32-of-pixels tops out at **kNN 0.21**, so **no store on those addresses can win** (N102). *Why it matters:* it gates the whole CL programme off MNIST. *Order:* the existing **`ae` arm** first, then a small **conv/SSL** φ fit on **task-1 classes only** (`phi_dim ≥ 16`). ⭐ **Gate before any entry run: does kNN-in-φ on Split-CIFAR-10 clear 0.35 (from 0.21)? A miss is the finding — stop and report it.** ⚠ **Trap that must be stated in the write-up: a better φ raises the launder by construction** — this buys a *viable* entry, not a *non-laundered* one. Status: **`TASKED(cl-encoder)` (w26).**
- **⭐⭐ The forgetting-vs-BYTES frontier — the program's nearest primary-claim candidate.** *What we haven't shown:* a Pareto frontier of forgetting/BWT against **bytes** (not items), across ≥5 budget points, against tuned **ER / DER++ / GDumb / iCaRL** at every point. *Why it matters:* four-for-four laundering means the primary claim cannot come from any metric where the store approximates a lookup — but **replay does not fail by construction**, and CLU already forgets *less* than ER at the same item budget with **24.5× fewer floats**. *"Accuracy at matched memory is laundered; forgetting at matched bytes is not."* ⛔ **Byte accounting must be pinned and published first, and the launder must run at matched BYTES too** (the ring buffer gets its ~24.5× keys — anything less is rigged). ⭐ **Where the store saturates is a reported result, not a failure** (the capacity law showing up inside a benchmark). Status: **`TASKED(matched-bytes-frontier)` (w26)** — the contested-win experiment.
- **⭐ PGCP's landing, and its rematch cell under the REAL read.** *What we haven't shown:* the canonical placement rule **in shipped code** (`Controller.delete` does not exist), and its admission/retention numbers under the **two-phase Verlet read** rather than gradient-flow relaxation. ⛔ **Until the rematch runs, "0.953" is not a measured retention number.** ⭐ **The acceptance criterion is a number, not a description: drive post-evict `AUC(z_hole)` from 0.99985 to ~0.5 on the analyst's MIA harness.** Also owed in the same landing: the **D1 array-scrub** (`evict` must clear `centers`/`payloads`), an LRU guard under `placement="canonical"`, and the sizing multiplier (**1.05 gives `N_cells ≥ K` for K ∈ {16,32,64,128}**; K=8 is small-K discrete, needs ≈1.35). Status: **`TASKED(placement-landing)` (w26)**; the **P2 waitlist** (overflow counterfactual exactness — specified, proof sketched, not implemented) remains `OPEN`.
- **⭐ The occupancy sweep on the allocator trace — it scopes a headline number AND an acceptance criterion.** *What we haven't shown:* how the post-evict hole signal behaves away from near-full load. **0.99985 was measured at 7/8 of the packing bound — the most favourable case for a hole statistic**; the leak may be far weaker at low occupancy, which would *usefully* scope the claim. ⚠ **File discipline: copy the MIA harness — do not edit the one `placement-landing` depends on.** Status: **`TASKED(carried-remeasurements)` (w26).**
- **The payload-dependent-lifetime fix (D3) — a theorist ruling BEFORE any potential change.** *What we haven't shown:* whether the lifetimes dial can be made payload-independent, and at what cost. Today effective half-life correlates **r = −0.85 with `a_i²`** (retention at the floor 0.999 for `a = ±0.1429` vs **0.500 for `a = +1`**) because the `q₂(0) = 0` guard makes each site a payload **hill** of height `0.5κa_i²` against a well of depth `A`. *Candidate fixes:* scale the payload bump by the amplitude (`payloads * amps`); launch reads at `q₂ = S(q_addr)`; ⭐ or **Advisor-2's cheap calibration option — the user sets a half-life and the store solves for the per-item leak, leaving the potential untouched.** *Why it matters:* it is the sentence a referee attacks first in any "set-at-write-time lifetimes" claim. ⛔ **Do not change the potential before the ruling.** Status: **`TASKED(readout-channel-theory` Q3`)` (w26)**; the re-measurement afterwards is `OPEN`.
- **Multi-seed of the τ-not-binding observation — a 1-seed result that contradicts w24.** *What we haven't shown:* whether *"τ is not the binding element of the gate in φ-space"* survives replication. In w24 τ=1.0 demonstrably over-retried; in the φ-space CL store all four thresholds give **identical** curves. *Why it matters:* the gate's threshold clause is quoted as load-bearing in the R3 story, and in φ-space it may not be. Status: **`TASKED(carried-remeasurements)` (w26).**
- **The LwF re-tune, and three thin-seed re-checks.** (i) ⚠ **LwF is the one CL baseline not reproduced** (19.6 vs published 23.9, 4.3 pp low) — **it must be re-tuned before the baseline table is published**, and it matters *because it is the method CLU loses to on CIFAR*. (ii) A **3-seed repeat of the Stage-0 decisive cells** (d=6 K=64, d=8 K=64), whose widths are currently 1 seed. (iii) A **5-seed re-check of the d=4 K=32 RG band** (0.89–0.90 sits closer to the bar than pre-registered). Status: `OPEN` (small; (i) blocks a table, (ii)/(iii) block digits, not verdicts).
- **Two smaller boundaries the wave named and nobody owns yet.** (a) **The designed arm at half excursion was NOT measured**, so the designed/learned tax at that task point is unknown — *`4·2^4 = 64` is an arithmetic coincidence until it is run.* (b) **`permanent_per_task` is an unstudied knob with an obvious capacity trade** (permanent items never leave — 10/task ⇒ 48 of 200 slots by end of stream; with no cap the store fills with permanent items and correctly refuses writes). A sweep turns *"the dial exists"* into *"the dial has a measured price curve."* Status: `OPEN` (both cheap).
- **⚠ Positioning debt created this wave: is "deletion cost at matched utility" already occupied?** *What we haven't shown:* that the second contested-win candidate is unclaimed. Also owed from the same task: **verify Naor–Teague / Blelloch–Golovin / Hartline / Micciancio / Karger from primaries** (currently model-knowledge-only and **load-bearing** on PGCP's novelty sentence), survey the **unlearning arena with per-deletion cost**, and **re-confirm the "certified" ban**. ⚠ The *expected* finding — **the discrete skeleton IS prior art** — is stated in advance so it is reported as expected, not as a surprise. Status: **`TASKED(deletion-prior-art)` (w26)** — ⛔ runs **before** any drafting; gates `placement-landing`'s wording (not its build).
